It moved DMA fence accounting from broad all-command waits to host-read waits in those scenes (`all=0 / host=15` field, `all=0 / host=15` menu, `all=0 / host=74` battle) while keeping sampled fused resolve/blit and GPU present-upload credit active. This is a small Windows `gpu-migration-credit`/sync-narrowing result, not a 200% performance proof and not an Android/Thor port candidate.
- Windows cached source-resolve probe 2026-05-19: `VerifyCachedSampled` field run `20260519-221519-rsx-blitsource-verifycachedsampled-field-v2-windows-windows` rendered correctly after the scratch layout fix, but verify mode fell back to the normal visible path and showed `blit_cache(hit/miss/fill/fanout/reject)=0/4376/4376/4376/0`. The actual fast cached path is better: `FastCachedSampled + DepthReadOnly + KeepReadOnly + GpuSwap + Host` reached clean field, menu/pause, and first-battle visuals in `20260519-223810-rsx-blitsource-fastcachedsampled-field-windows-windows`, `20260519-224209-rsx-blitsource-fastcachedsampled-menu-windows-windows`, and `20260519-224632-rsx-blitsource-fastcachedsampled-battle-windows-windows`. Cache counters matched the 66.67% duplicate source-tag scout (`8644/4322/4322/12966/0` field, `11104/5552/5552/16656/0` menu, `4372/2186/2186/6558/0` battle). Treat `FastCachedSampled` as Windows `gpu-migration-credit`, not a speed proof; the next proof needs matched uncapped movement against `FastSampled`.
- 2026-05-16 Rocknix 26 FPS delta: local Rocknix `next` checkout packages `rpcs3-sa` as RPCS3 Linux ARM64 AppImage `0.0.40-19291-d773a3f9`. Treat the video result as a real AYN Thor Rocknix/RPCS3 ARM target at **720p around 26 FPS**, not as a 480p preset run. Android low-res Rocknix-style diagnostic tests did **not** close the gap: `RocknixCorrect` at 480p/scale-50 on stock Qualcomm hit about `18.49 FPS`, `RocknixFast` with WCB off hit about `19.17 FPS`, and Android Turnip A7xx dropped to about `12.37 FPS`. Treat the remaining 720p gap as a runtime/system-driver/scheduler/core problem first, not a simple resolution/WCB/shader-precision settings miss. Use `Rocknix720Correct` / `Rocknix720Fast` in `tools/push_eternal_sonata_thor_profile.ps1` for direct Android-vs-Rocknix-video comparisons. Details live in `debug-experiments/20260516-rocknix-26fps-delta.md`.
- Control device logging with `.\tools\set_thor_logging.ps1 -Mode Quiet|Normal|Verbose|ReducedLoop|ReducedLoopEmit|ReducedLoopEmitQuiet|ReducedLoopEmitU4|ReducedLoopEmitU4Quiet|ReducedLoopEmitU8|ReducedLoopEmitU8Quiet|SpursProbe|SemaProfile|SemaFast|DmaProfile|DmaVerify|GpuSuperpathScout|RsxAuditor|RsxDmaHostFence|Status`. Use `Quiet` for stock FPS/performance sweeps, `ReducedLoopEmitU4Quiet` for low-overhead u4 FPS/performance sweeps, `Normal` for ordinary crash/boot repros, `ReducedLoop` only for short SPU compiler captures, noisy `ReducedLoopEmit*` modes only to debug the gated reduced-loop LLVM path, `SpursProbe` only for short SPURS/loading-hang captures, `SemaProfile`/`SemaFast` only for the gated Eternal Sonata semaphore probe, `DmaProfile`/`DmaVerify` only for short Eternal Sonata SPU DMA/MFC candidate runs, `GpuSuperpathScout` for short paired DMA-verify plus RSX-auditor captures when evaluating CPU-to-GPU offload viability, `RsxAuditor` only for short RSX/Vulkan barrier/pipeline/tiling captures, `RsxDmaHostFence` only for short correctness/FPS tests of the experimental RSX DMA host-read fence, and `Verbose` only for short targeted syscall captures because it enables PPU syscall usage stats.
- Summarize `RsxAuditor` captures with `.\tools\summarize_eternal_sonata_rsx_auditor.ps1 -RunDir RUN_DIR`. Use its pressure mix to separate pipeline warmup, DMA transfer fences, render-pass/tile-locality breaks, hard sync drains, and upload/detile traffic before deciding whether the next RSX/GPU slice should be fence narrowing, barrier locality, or true GPU-resident data prep.
- Windows `rpcs3-upstream` RSX lab patch is controlled by process env through `tools/windows_rpcs3_lab.ps1`: `-RsxAuditor On|frame|N`, `-RsxDmaFence Host`, `-RsxTextureBarrier Depth|DepthReadOnly|Color|All`, `-RsxDepthFeedback KeepReadOnly`, `-RsxResolve Profile|SkipColor|SkipDepth|SkipAll`, `-RsxBlitSourceResolve Verify|VerifySampled|VerifyCachedSampled|Fast|FastSampled|FastCachedSampled|FastKeepSrc`, `-RsxPresentUpload GpuSwap`, and diagnostic `-RsxIndexUpload GpuSwap` map to `RPCS3_ES_RSX_AUDITOR`, `RPCS3_ES_RSX_DMA_FENCE`, `RPCS3_ES_RSX_TEXTURE_BARRIER`, `RPCS3_ES_RSX_DEPTH_FEEDBACK`, `RPCS3_ES_RSX_RESOLVE`, `RPCS3_ES_RSX_BLIT_SOURCE_RESOLVE`, `RPCS3_ES_RSX_PRESENT_UPLOAD`, and `RPCS3_ES_RSX_INDEX_UPLOAD`. `-RsxForceHwMsaaResolve On|Off|Keep` / sprint wrapper `-WindowsRsxForceHwMsaaResolve` temporarily edits RPCS3's `Force Hardware MSAA Resolve` setting and restores it after the run. First Windows field result on 2026-05-17 showed baseline render-pass breaks dominated by image+depth texture barriers (`g/b/i/t=0/0/3169/1585`); `Depth` skip removed texture breaks and left image breaks. Follow-up labels showed those remaining image render-pass breaks come from `render_target::resolve` (`img_break unk/rt_res/rt_unres/rt_post/rt_other/tc/draw/pres/tex/up=0/3381/0/0/0/0/0/0/0/0` in the refined Windows run). On 2026-05-18, `SkipColor` removed the resolve breaks (`rp_break=0`, `resolve skips=6876`) but the field rendered almost entirely black, so do not port/enable resolve skipping. Non-destructive `Profile` runs found one hot color target (`1280x720`, `fmt=0x2c`, `samples=2`, `grid=2x1`, `pitch=10240`, `base=0xc0b20000`) with zero duplicate last-use tags, so simple duplicate-skip/debounce is not viable. The reason-profile run `20260518-103121-rsx-resolve-reason-profile-windows` showed the hot resolves are `transfer-read` from `render_target::memory_barrier`, not spill or memory-copy. The `20260518-132528-rsx-force-hw-msaa-resolve-windows` A/B reduced color resolve pressure but introduced visible black square sprites around field flowers/foliage and a new depth resolve stream, so reject this config as a speed route. The next valid RSX route is transfer-read consumer callsite labeling and a narrower texture-consumer locality experiment, not global hardware MSAA resolve or dropping resolves. Details live in `debug-experiments/20260518-rsx-gpu-residency-plan.md`.
- Windows transfer-consumer profile slice 2026-05-18: local `rpcs3-upstream` labels `surface_access::transfer_read` consumers with resolve reasons such as `texture-fbo-sample`, `present`, `texture-cache-lock`, `blit-source`, and `old-content-copy-source`; the Android summarizer maps those numeric reasons. Clean baseline run `20260518-134854-rsx-transfer-consumer-baseline-windows` showed the hot resolve consumer is `blit-source`, still the same single `1280x720` 2x1 color target (`fmt=0x2c`, `base=0xc0b20000`, dup tags `0`), at `3402` resolves / `7200` frames (`28.35` per 60). With texture-barrier skip off, field screenshots at the same camera were clean; the paired depth-skip run `20260518-134503-rsx-transfer-consumer-profile-windows` reduced texture breaks but showed black-backed flower/foliage billboards, so treat the old depth-skip route as correctness-suspect. The next RSX-on-GPU experiment should target the `texture_cache::upload_scaled_image` source-render-target blit path: profile blit src/dst geometry, then try a title-gated fused MSAA-resolve-plus-blit or renderpass-local resolve path, with field/menu/battle screenshots before any Thor port.
- Windows blit-source geometry/fused-resolve slice 2026-05-18: `tools/windows_rpcs3_lab.ps1 -RsxBlitSourceResolve Verify|Fast` / sprint wrapper `-WindowsRsxBlitSourceResolve Verify|Fast` maps to `RPCS3_ES_RSX_BLIT_SOURCE_RESOLVE=verify|fast`, and the summarizer emits `eternal-sonata-rsx-blit-source-profile.csv` plus `blit_resolve(fast/verify/reject)` and reject-reason counters. Geometry run `20260518-141204-rsx-blit-source-geometry-profile-windows` confirmed the hot `blit-source` consumer is a stable RSX-local candidate around the `1280x720`, `fmt=0x2c`, `2x1` MSAA source at `0xc0b20000`; Windows RPCS3 was routed to `\\.\DISPLAY2`. The fused fast prototype dropped render-pass breaks/resolves to zero, but every visual run rendered a black field, including solid-magenta diagnostic writes and untyped/storage-destination variants (`20260518-143950`, `144937`, `145729`, `150422`, `151129`, `151717`). A live-destination verify/storage attempt also black-screened and hit image-layout validation (`20260518-172519-rsx-blit-source-verify-storage-windows`). The scratch verify run `20260518-173457-rsx-blit-source-verify-scratch-windows` kept the field visually correct and dispatched `3,458` candidate GPU resolve/blits (`28.70` per 60 frames) while the normal path still rendered; rejects were all non-MSAA/non-require-resolve render-target cases. Later Windows fast field/menu/battle screenshots showed the shader-read-layout fix can keep visuals correct while dispatching fused resolves, but this is still only a correctness foothold. Under the 2026-05-18 user gate, do not port or run this path on Thor/Android until matched Windows runs show a stable 200%+ moving-gameplay performance improvement with correct field, menu, and first-battle visuals.
- Windows depth-feedback RSX slice 2026-05-18: `tools/windows_rpcs3_lab.ps1 -RsxDepthFeedback KeepReadOnly` / sprint wrapper `-WindowsRsxDepthFeedback KeepReadOnly` maps to `RPCS3_ES_RSX_DEPTH_FEEDBACK=keep-readonly`. The summarizer now reports `depth_feedback(prep_keep/prep_layout/prep_write/end_keep/end_layout/end_write/end_restore)` and texture-barrier `bound` flags. Texture profiling found the hot bound read-only depth surface at `0xc1260000`, `fmt=0x81`, `1280x720`, `samples=2`, `grid=2x1`, `pitch=10240`, with desired feedback-loop layout `1000339000`. Destructive `-RsxTextureBarrier Depth` collapses render-pass breaks from about `36` to about `6` per 60 frames but is correctness-suspect due earlier black foliage/flower billboard artifacts and still is not a Windows speed win. Safe `KeepReadOnly` preserve/prime probes stayed visually correct in field and first battle, but matched counters stayed around `36.5` render-pass breaks and `7.9k` depth texture barriers per full route. New Windows-only `-RsxTextureBarrier DepthReadOnly` / `-WindowsRsxTextureBarrier DepthReadOnly` maps to `RPCS3_ES_RSX_TEXTURE_BARRIER=depth-readonly` and narrows the skip to BLUS30161's exact read-only depth feedback-loop surface (`fmt=0x81`, `1280x720`, `2x1`, `pitch=10240`, bound, render pass open). Clean field-to-first-battle run `20260518-222010-rsx-texturebarrier-depthreadonly-fast-uncap-vblank240-fieldbattle-windows-windows` kept battle/tutorial visuals correct and reduced render-pass breaks to `1594` / `7.89` per 60 frames with `4364` forced skips on that one surface. The menu route is still noisy: broad `Depth` reached a correct Options page, while narrowed `DepthReadOnly` reached field/pause/cutscene skip visuals but did not reliably reach the full Options page. Treat this as the best current RSX locality foothold, not a promotable optimization; do not port to Android/Thor before the 200% Windows moving-gameplay gate is met. Full notes live in `debug-experiments/20260518-rsx-gpu-residency-plan.md`.
- Windows resolve-barrier split slice 2026-05-18: local `rpcs3-upstream` auditor now logs `resolve_barrier(...)` and `resolve_break(...)` for normal RT resolve and fused blit-source resolve. DepthReadOnly + Fast fieldbattle run `20260518-232448-rsx-resolve-barrier-profile-depthreadonly-fast-fieldbattle-windows-windows` kept first-battle visuals correct and showed the remaining counted `rt_res` render-pass breaks are all `blit_src_to_general` (`resolve_break=0/0/0/0/1598/0/0`). DepthReadOnly + FastKeepSrc run `20260518-232743-rsx-resolve-barrier-profile-depthreadonly-fastkeepsrc-fieldbattle-windows-windows` stayed visually correct and removed most non-breaking restore transitions (`resolve_barrier=0/0/0/0/1602/0/4806`) but left the actual break count flat (`1600`), so source-keep is not a speed lane by itself. `Force Hardware MSAA Resolve` re-check `20260518-233341-rsx-forcehwmsaa-depthreadonly-fast-fieldbattle-windows-windows` routed into story/cutscene, drove GPU utilization high, and worsened resolve/break counters, so do not chase global hardware MSAA resolve. The next RSX-only idea must be renderpass-local/tile-local resolve/blit architecture, not more one-dispatch compute blits; SPU `0x25cc`/`0x451c` codegen/HLE remains the stronger near-term CPU-load target.
- The logging tool sets Android properties over ADB: `debug.rpcsx.thor.logcat`, `debug.rpcsx.thor.syscall_stats`, `debug.rpcsx.thor.spu_reduced_loop_detect`, `debug.rpcsx.thor.spu_reduced_loop_emit`, `debug.rpcsx.thor.spurs_probe`, `debug.rpcsx.thor.es_sema_superpath`, `debug.rpcsx.thor.es_dma_superpath`, `debug.rpcsx.thor.rsx_auditor`, `debug.rpcsx.thor.rsx_dma_fence`, `debug.rpcsx.thor.dump_prx`, `log.tag.RPCS3`, and `log.tag.RPCSX-UI`. The bundled core polls/logging properties at runtime where supported, so Codex can turn logcat/syscall-stat pressure on or off without rebuilding after this APK is installed.
- Important logging fix: `tools/set_thor_logging.ps1` must reset `debug.rpcsx.thor.rsx_dma_fence=off` for every non-`RsxDmaHostFence` mode. Earlier quiet/u4 runs before the fix could accidentally leave the experimental RSX DMA host-fence path active, so do not compare them as clean stock unless the captured props show `rsx_dma_fence=off`.
- `tools/eternal_sonata_speed_sprint.ps1 -Action AndroidScene|AndroidRouteScene` now captures `cpu-freq.txt`, `gpu-freq.txt`, and `thread-affinity.txt` through the shared Thor snapshot helper. Use those files for Rocknix delta work: the next suspect is CPU governor/core placement, Adreno frequency/busy behavior, and RSX/SPU/PPU thread placement rather than another 480p settings sweep.
- Live affinity A/B helper: `.\tools\set_thor_runtime_affinity.ps1 -Mode Status|DefaultF8|AllThreadsFF|RsxPrimeSpuNonPrimePpuA715`, or pass `-AndroidRuntimeAffinityMode` with the same modes to `tools/eternal_sonata_speed_sprint.ps1` scene captures. `AllThreadsFF` gave a small live field bump from about `20.35` to `21.40 FPS`; `RsxPrimeSpuNonPrimePpuA715` got worse around `19.01 FPS`. Always restore `DefaultF8` after ad hoc tests unless the next capture explicitly labels the changed affinity mode.
- `Quiet`, `Normal`, `Verbose`, `ReducedLoop`, `ReducedLoopEmit`, and `SpursProbe` all clear `debug.rpcsx.thor.dump_prx=0` and reset `debug.rpcsx.thor.es_sema_superpath=off`; use the dedicated Ghidra PRX workflow when a decrypted module dump is needed.
- Do not leave `debug.rpcsx.thor.syscall_stats=1` during FPS testing. Eternal Sonata can hit hundreds of thousands of timer/semaphore/SPU-group syscalls in a few minutes, and stats/logging can become its own benchmark poison.
- Live debug loop while the user plays: `.\tools\start_thor_debug_stream.ps1 -ClearLogcat -Launch -Label GAME`, then repeatedly run `.\tools\summarize_thor_debug_stream.ps1 -Latest` from Codex while the user reproduces the issue, then `.\tools\stop_thor_debug_stream.ps1 -Latest`.
- Live streams write to ignored `debug-captures/*-stream/` folders. Watch `summary-latest.md`, `logcat-live.txt`, `rpcsx-live-tail.txt`, and `live-summary/now-*.txt`.
- Newer live streams also write `memory-live.txt`; use it to catch RSS/swap growth before Android's low-memory killer takes the app down.
- Clean repro flow: `.\tools\collect_thor_debug.ps1 -Prepare -Launch -Label GAME`, reproduce on Thor, then `.\tools\collect_thor_debug.ps1 -Label GAME`.
- Do not run `-Prepare` after a crash; it clears logcat and destroys the freshest evidence.
- Captures are written under ignored `debug-captures/` folders. Start analysis with `logcat-interesting.txt`, `device-files/cache/RPCSX.log`, `rpcsx-log-errors.txt`, `activity.txt`, and `cache-summary.txt`.
- Classify the failure before changing code: native/app crash, black screen while app is alive, or regression after settings/core/cache/driver/cheat changes.
- For black screen while alive, check whether the title is still compiling, installing game data under `dev_hdd0/game`, waiting on a PS3 dialog, or producing audio without RSX frames.
- Reusable Ghidra pattern for game-specific hangs: first capture logs/probes until they name a guest module and address, then dump/decompile that exact decrypted PRX instead of guessing from raw firmware files. Raw `*.sprx` files are often encrypted `SCE\0` containers and are usually the wrong input.
- Use `.\tools\run_thor_ghidra_prx_probe.ps1 -Module NAME -Addresses 0xADDR1,0xADDR2 -WaitSeconds N` when a log points at code such as `libsre:0x00cc948c`. The helper sets `debug.rpcsx.thor.dump_prx=NAME`, waits for the core-side decrypted `prog.prx` dump under the app cache, imports it into a timestamped Ghidra project, and writes decompile output under `debug-captures/ghidra-NAME-*`.
- Treat Ghidra output as a map for the next instrumented build, not as proof by itself. Convert findings into narrow probes around the specific syscall, SPURS state, reservation address, mailbox/channel, or guest loop that the decompile exposes; then rebuild/install and verify on Thor.
- Use Ghidra primarily for guest-code questions such as "what is this hot SPURS handler doing?" or "what condition keeps this loading loop alive?" For Android/native tombstones in `librpcsx-android.so`, start with tombstone/logcat/addr2line-style native symbol work first, then use Ghidra only if the crash is tied back to a guest PRX/module address.
- After each Ghidra-assisted investigation, add a dated AGENTS note with the capture folder, module, addresses, observed guest behavior, and the next probe target. This keeps the thread from repeating the same expensive dump/decompile loop.
- If a live stream is active, do not leave it running forever after the repro. Stop it, inspect the final pull, then commit fixes or notes.
- A 2026-05-12 Eternal Sonata live stream at roughly 10-13 FPS showed no OOM/crash, but did show `rsx::thread` plus six SPU threads hot, heavy `sys_timer_usleep`, `sys_semaphore_wait/post`, `sys_event_queue_receive`, and repeated `sys_spu_thread_group_start/join`. Treat this as a SPURS/RSX contention profile first, not a basic boot failure.
- A 2026-05-12 Eternal Sonata `Now Loading` hang after reduced-loop capture was app-alive, not an Android crash: no ANR/tombstone, about 118% process CPU, about 2.2 GB PSS, with `rsx::thread`, `SPU[0x0000200]`, and multiple PPU threads hot. If this repeats, switch to `Quiet`, sample `top -H`/meminfo, then force-stop; do not leave `ReducedLoop` logging on during load/FPS testing.
- A later 2026-05-12 stuck `Now Loading` capture in `Quiet` showed active loading-screen animation, no crash, stable memory, and a repeating SPURS workload churn: in 10 seconds roughly 102k `sys_timer_usleep`, 4.2k `sys_event_queue_receive`, 3.7k semaphore waits/posts, and 1.6k `sys_spu_thread_group_start/join`. Use the gated `SpursProbe` mode to identify the exact SPU group/name/cause/status plus SPU-side LR/notifier/PUTLLC wait counters before changing SPURS scheduling or reservation waits. Code inspection found upstream RPCS3 waits on generation-specific reservation notifier values, while the Android fork still waits on a constant waiter flag in this path; treat that as a prime suspect, but do not re-port the rtime notifier blindly because the first attempt caused a deterministic SIGBUS.
- A 2026-05-12 `SpursProbe` capture on the rebuilt wait-probe APK at Eternal Sonata `Now Loading` showed 23 `Thor SPURS probe` group-join samples and **zero** `Thor SPURS wait probe` samples. The hot loop is `PPU[0x1000009] SpursHdlr0` at `libsre:0x00cc948c` repeatedly starting/joining single-SPU `CellSpursKernelGroup` `0x4000200` (`max_num=1`, `max_run=1`, `cause=0x1`, `status=0`, about 94 joins/sec), while syscall stats still show heavy `sys_timer_usleep`, event-queue receives, semaphore wait/post, and SPU group start/join. Next probe should move to PPU-side event/semaphore/SPURS workload state or group args/exit context; do not assume the hang is an SPU LR/notifier/PUTLLC wait.
- Imported probe slice 2026-05-13: `SpursProbe` now also logs `Thor SPURS PPU wait probe` once per second from PPU-side `sys_event_queue_receive`, `sys_semaphore_wait/post`, and `sys_timer_usleep`. It reports cumulative counts plus PPU id/name, CIA/LR, object id, timeout, details, and result. This is read-only instrumentation for the Eternal Sonata SPURS churn; use it to decide whether the hot `libsre` handler is primarily sleeping, waiting on a specific queue/semaphore, or bouncing through ready events before changing scheduler behavior.
- Ghidra path for static analysis is `C:\Users\leanerdesigner\Documents\SteamPortableTools\toolchains\ghidra_12.0.4_PUBLIC`; the local headless helper is `.\tools\run_thor_ghidra_prx_probe.ps1`. For the Eternal Sonata loading hang, set/dump `libsre` and decompile `0x00cc948c` / `0x00cc945c`. Raw firmware `libsre.sprx` is an `SCE\0` container and does not import directly into Ghidra; use the gated core property `debug.rpcsx.thor.dump_prx=libsre` so the emulator dumps the decrypted PRX after `decrypt_self`, then pull/run Ghidra from `debug-captures/`.
- For decrypted `libsre` Ghidra imports, do not assume the logged guest address is the raw Ghidra import address. The 2026-05-15 probe showed the useful text bytes at a `0x100` skew in the import, and the Eternal Sonata `SpursHdlr0` run appeared to use a module base near `0x00cc0000`: logged `libsre:0x00cc930c` mapped to Ghidra `0x0000940c`, and logged `libsre:0x00cc948c` mapped to Ghidra `0x0000958c`. If Ghidra reports bad instruction data, recalculate `guest_address - module_base + import_skew` before trusting the miss.
- Game-specific "super speed" paths are allowed only as emulator-side, correctness-locked, title/signature-gated experiments. For Eternal Sonata, the first candidate is a `BLUS30161` SPURS handler superpath around the `libsre` `SpursHdlr0` loop that repeatedly starts/joins single-SPU `CellSpursKernelGroup` work (`max_num=1`, `max_run=1`, `cause=0x1`, `status=0`). Keep it behind a debug property until field/battle/menu visual checks and Thor FPS prove it.
- Windows lab superpath slice 2026-05-15: `rpcs3-upstream` now has a gated Eternal Sonata SPURS detector/cache path in `sys_spu` controlled by `RPCS3_ES_SPURS_SUPERPATH=detect|cache`, exposed through `tools/windows_rpcs3_lab.ps1 -EternalSonataSuperPath Detect|Cache` and the sprint wrapper. It matches `BLUS30161`, `SpursHdlr0`/`TCX_SpursHdlr0`, `CellSpursKernelGroup`, `max_num=1`, `max_run=1`; cache mode materializes 1,920 bytes of SPU image segments after normal deploy proves no patches applied, then reuses those bytes on later starts. It does not skip SPU execution, `cpu_init()`, or join cause/status.
- Windows result for that slice: detect/cache runs under `debug-captures/windows-lab/20260515-192807-eternal-sonata-superpath-detect/`, `20260515-192925-eternal-sonata-superpath-cache/`, and `20260515-194147-eternal-sonata-superpath-cache-final/` confirmed about 94 start/join cycles/sec, one cache build followed by thousands of hits, and no title/preload visual corruption. The deploy/init cost is tiny on Windows; cumulative join/work wait dominates, so this cache path is a safe probe but probably not the dramatic FPS cure by itself.
- Windows GPU-candidate probe slice 2026-05-15: `rpcs3-upstream` now has `RPCS3_ES_GPU_PROBE=profile`, exposed through `tools/windows_rpcs3_lab.ps1 -EternalSonataGpuProbe Profile` and summarized by `tools/summarize_eternal_sonata_gpu_probe.ps1`. Field route run `debug-captures/windows-lab/20260515-230825-eternal-sonata-gpu-probe-field-sampled-windows/` reached 3D gameplay with FPS overlay and no obvious field visual corruption, but host contention was `high` because Vita3K was active. The sampled probe found 1,157 records, about 1.35 GB observed DMA, top image `0x958dfe208b686622`, hot PCs `0x25cc` and `0x451c`, and zero RSX-local traffic. Later smoke `debug-captures/windows-lab/20260515-232647-eternal-sonata-gpu-probe-disasm-smoke-windows/` also dumped `spu-images/BLUS30161-spu-image-958dfe208b686622-entry-00818-...ls.bin` plus hot-PC disassembly sidecars. The 2026-05-18 field-to-first-battle Windows scout `debug-captures/windows-lab/20260518-212026-gpu-candidate-probe-fieldbattle-windows-windows/` reached field and active first-battle tutorial visuals; it saw 670 records, about 898.25 MB observed DMA, hot PCs `0x451c`/`0x25cc`, zero direct RSX-local records, and zero indirect overlap between sampled SPU DMA/MFC ranges and 3,093 profiled RSX resource rows. `0x25cc` and `0x451c` both sit in MFC/DMA command issue/wait code, so the first drastic candidate is a verified SPU DMA-loop/job-HLE, reduced-loop/codegen, or scheduler/copy superpath, not a blind Vulkan compute offload, unless a later menu/battle/RenderDoc/AGI capture proves GPU-consumed output.
- Optional Windows/Android probe `RPCS3_ES_SPURS_JOIN_SPIN=N` / `-EternalSonataJoinSpin N` exists but defaults off. The Windows spin run `debug-captures/windows-lab/20260515-193614-eternal-sonata-superpath-cache-spin/` had zero spin hits and all misses, slightly worsening join timing. Do not enable it for FPS sweeps unless a Thor trace shows Android-specific scheduler latency short enough to catch.
- The latest SPURS state probe logs `Thor SPURS state probe` once per second for `CellSpursKernelGroup` joins. Use it with `SpursProbe` to capture the SPURS control block/workload state alongside the existing join loop before changing SPURS scheduling or event/semaphore semantics.
- Before trusting an Eternal Sonata FPS result, inspect `config/custom_configs/config_BLUS30161.yml`. A stale full custom config can override Thor defaults, but the attempted managed `Max SPURS Threads: 4` profile caused a black-screen-alive load hang on 2026-05-12. Use `.\tools\push_eternal_sonata_thor_profile.ps1` only as a safe boot rollback; do not reapply SPURS 4 for this game without a separate instrumented test build.
- A live Thor repro on 2026-05-11 caught `BLUS31386` crashing in PPU LLVM precompile with `memory_commit(... errno=12=Out of memory)` and an LLVM `report_bad_alloc_error` tombstone. Treat first-boot full PPU precompilation as risky on Thor; the fork defaults should prefer `LLVM Precompilation=false` and low compile-thread pressure unless a guarded cache-builder mode is being tested.
- A later 2026-05-11 repro showed Android LMK killing `net.rpcsx.easy` near 6 GB RSS while a direct ISO game opened multi-GB `/dev_bdvd/PS3_GAME/USRDIR/archives/*.files` payloads. The vendored ISO device must stream file reads; do not reintroduce whole-file ISO reads or whole-file ISO extraction copies for large game data.

## Device Testing

## Windows PS3 / RSX Lab Notes

- Stay Windows-only for PS3 GPU migration work until a stable 200%+ Windows moving-gameplay win is proven with clean field, menu/Options, and first-battle visuals. Do not port RSX/SPU GPU experiments to Thor before that gate.
- Always route Windows RPCS3/PS3 gameplay to the second screen with `-WindowsGameScreen 1`.
- 2026-05-20 route correction: the old Eternal Sonata Windows scene macro was accidentally selecting title Options instead of loading the field. Use short PadApi title pulses: `down:20;wait:500;cross:80`, then select Save File 01, move to Yes, acknowledge `Load complete`.
- `tools/windows_rpcs3_lab.ps1` now supports labeled macro screenshots such as `shot:load-list` and writes names like `screenshot-0060s-load-list.png`. Use labeled shots at every route branch.
- 2026-05-20 save-list route correction: the Windows scene macro now sends repeated discrete `up` pulses on the Load save list before pressing `cross`, because RPCS3/Eternal Sonata can remember a lower save slot such as Save File 04 from a prior failed route. Do not put the save-select `cross` before the save-list normalization step in custom macros.
- `tools/eternal_sonata_speed_sprint.ps1` supports `-WindowsCpuAffinityMask`, passed to `tools/windows_rpcs3_lab.ps1` as `-CpuAffinityMask`, for Windows-only constrained-CPU A/B tests. Example: `-WindowsCpuAffinityMask 0x0F` pins RPCS3 to the first four logical CPUs and logs the applied mask.
- Current true-load route reaches the Path to Tenuto field with `-WindowsGameScreen 1`. If a run stalls on the Load screen with `Load complete.`, the final Cross probably fired before the prompt was ready; use the current `tools/eternal_sonata_speed_sprint.ps1` field route, which waits longer before the final acknowledge.
- 2026-05-20 `FastCachedTransferSampled` true-field A/B: it is real GPU migration credit, not a speed win. True field `FastCachedTransferSampled` produced `blit_cache_transfer_src(fill/fanout)=17559/52677` and clean visuals, while matched `FastSampled` had no cache transfer-src work. The screenshot FPS point was lower for cached-transfer than control, so this is not a `windows-micro-win` and nowhere near the 200% gate.
- 2026-05-20 constrained-CPU check: under `-WindowsCpuAffinityMask 0x0F`, clean field checkpoints were reached for `FastSampled` and `FastCachedTransferSampled`, but cached-transfer still did not prove a speed win. Final field overlay was about `42.66` FPS for `FastSampled` versus about `29.86` FPS for cached-transfer, while RPCS3 CPU stayed about equal (`26.2` versus `26.1`) and host GPU engine use rose (`20.1` to `26.7`). Smaller-CPU benefit remains plausible for paths that actually remove CPU work, but this specific path is only `gpu-migration-credit`.
- 2026-05-20 source-layout check: `FastKeepSrc` was visually clean in field but did not reduce the real `blit_src_to_general` render-pass break debt. It removed restore churn (`blit_src_restore` barriers went to zero) while actual source-read breaks stayed around `30` per 60 frames, so do not treat keep-source alone as a speed lane.
- 2026-05-22 source-writer profile rule: do not attach real GPU fill/resolve work to every `surface_store::on_write()` hot-source writer event. Clean moving field `20260522-120840-cpu4-source-writer-profile-circleclose-movingloop-windows` logged about `6,981,244` exact hot-source writer hits and about `6,970,024` hot fast writes, roughly `55k` hot writer events per 60 frames, while consumer-side source-read breaks stayed only `44-58` per 60 frames. The next RSX source-local design must coalesce by source tag/frame or attach near `memory_barrier(resolve_reason_blit_source)` / fused consumer demand; a naive producer-fill-per-write path is parked.
- 2026-05-22 source-writer tag scout gotcha: `20260522-123255-cpu4-source-writer-tag-profile-circleclose-movingloop-windows` and retry `20260522-123834-cpu4-source-writer-tag-profile-circleclose-movingloop-retry-windows` both built and logged the new `source_writer_tag(new/repeat/collision/zero)` tuple, but screenshots were black with only the overlay visible and hot-source writer/source-debt counters were zero. Treat these as `failed` / `visual-black` / `not-comparable`; do not use their zero hot/tag counts as proof that the source debt vanished. The next Windows-only step is an isolate control or a tag-scout rerun only after the correct field visuals return.
- 2026-05-22 source-writer tag scout resolved: profile-off control `20260522-125319-cpu4-source-writer-tag-control-profileoff-movingloop-windows` restored correct load/menu/field/movement visuals, and profile-on rerun `20260522-125925-cpu4-source-writer-tag-profile-rerun-after-clean-control-windows` also stayed visually clean. The valid profile run logged `5,427,066` hot writer hits, `303,104` new writer tags, `0` repeats, and `5,123,962` tag-table collisions; steady intervals hit the `8192` new-tag cap with about `141k` collisions per 60 frames. Treat writer-stage `write_tag` coalescing as parked/too-hot. The next RSX source-local experiment should attach at consumer demand or the resolve/source-base coalescer (`unique_tag_floor` about `4,358` per run / `37.6` per 60), not at every producer write.
- 2026-05-22 source-local fanout analysis: the valid profile run's source-local eligibility CSV has exactly three hot source chunks (`0xc0b20000`, `0xc0b21000`, `0xc0b22000`) and two destination images per chunk, `13,074` fully eligible rows total. This means a source-cache fanout can reduce `13,074` destination resolve/blit calls to roughly `4,358` source fills plus `8,716` copies, but the `4,356` remaining render-pass breaks line up with that unique source floor. Do not spend more loops on cached-source fanout tuning alone; the next RSX speed lane must avoid or move the first source-layout break itself, via renderpass-local/subpass-style source read, pre-stage before the render pass, or a precise `memory_barrier(resolve_reason_blit_source)`-adjacent verifier.
- 2026-05-22 source-fused tag verifier: local `rpcs3-upstream` now logs profile-only `source_fused_tag(new/repeat/collision/zero)` at the exact fused blit-source candidate boundary. Clean Windows moving-field run `20260522-134633-cpu4-source-fused-tag-profile-movingloop-windows` showed valid load/menu/field/movement screenshots and `source_fused_tag=4298/8596/0/0`, while `resolve_break_blit_src=4296` and resolve coalescing reported `blit_source_calls=12894`, `duplicate_tags=8596`, `unique_tag_floor=4298`. This confirms consumer-side source tags coalesce cleanly and the first source-tag fill/layout break is the target. Next RSX work should prototype a verify/profile renderpass-local or pre-stage source fill for the exact hot source tag, not producer writes or cached destination fanout.
- 2026-05-22 source-read rebind static gate: re-checking the Vulkan path after the fused-tag proof confirmed why `FastSampledKeepSrc` must stay parked. `VKRenderPass.cpp` render-pass keys reject `VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` for attachments, `prepare_surface_for_drawing(...)` rebinds color targets as `COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL`, and sampled `resolve_blit_image(...)` reads the real MSAA source in `SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL` only as a temporary compute-read layout. The next source-break experiment must either fill a separate cache/scratch image while restoring the real render target before rebind, or add an explicit rebind/render-pass-key legalization verifier before any keep-read fast path. Do not re-add a plain sampled keep-read/source-local toggle.
- 2026-05-22 begin-render-pass source prefill scout: local `rpcs3-upstream` now logs profile-only `source_prefill_begin(call/rp/bound/hot/resolve/tagdirty)` at `VKGSRender::begin_render_pass()`. The fast scout `20260522-141642-cpu4-begin-prefill-fastcachedsampled-movingloop-windows` black-screened and crashed/froze with a PPU access violation reading `0x4`; the profile-only scout `20260522-142049-cpu4-begin-prefill-profile-fastsampled-movingloop-windows` stayed error-clean but routed into cutscene/star visuals, not comparable moving field. Its counters still showed the begin hook is far too hot and usually already render-pass-open (`4713591/4638628/...`). Keep begin/natural-close prefill profile-only and parked; do not use `begin_render_pass()` or `close_render_pass()` as a fast source-prefill path. Next source-break work should be true renderpass-local/local-read or a precise `memory_barrier(resolve_reason_blit_source)` verifier.
- 2026-05-22 preserve-renderpass source barrier static gate: do not try a one-line `preserve_renderpass=true` change on the fused blit-source `blit_src_to_general` barrier. `vk::insert_image_memory_barrier(...)` would only avoid `vk::end_renderpass(...)`; the fused helper immediately runs a compute resolve/blit dispatch, and the current render pass is begun with inline graphics subpass contents. A legal source-break removal needs a real graphics/renderpass-local design, such as input-attachment/subpass feedback plumbing or a pre-pass scratch/cache fill before the dependent render pass opens, not compute dispatch inside an open render pass.
- 2026-05-22 framebuffer-transition source pre-stage verifier: `RPCS3_ES_RSX_SOURCE_PREFILL_PROFILE=profile` now also logs profile-only `source_transition(check/hot/retire/rp/resolve/tagdirty)` in `VKGSRender::prepare_rtts()`. Moving-field run `20260522-151821-cpu4-source-transition-profile-movingloop-windows` reached clean load/save/field/movement screenshots on `\\.\DISPLAY2` with no fatal/Vulkan/layout errors and logged `53913/12108/9081/12108/12108/12108`, while remaining source debt was `resolve_break_blit_src=6052` and `source_fused_tag(new/repeat)=6054/12108`. Because every hot transition was already render-pass-open, this hook is not a useful before-pass prefill point. Keep it profile-only and pivot RSX source-break work to real renderpass-local/local-read shader plumbing or a later exact `resolve_reason_blit_source` scheduling verifier.
- 2026-05-22 renderpass-local source-read static gate: the hot fused blit-source path is not a small input-attachment or render-pass-key patch. `VKResolveHelper.cpp::resolve_blit_image()` uses `cs_resolve_blit_task`, binds `sampler2DMS`/`image2DMS` plus a storage-image destination, transitions the source from color attachment to sampled/general layout, then runs `vkCmdDispatch`. The hot destination is a texture-cache `blit_engine_dst` image, not an FBO attachment. The Windows lab GPU supports `VK_EXT_attachment_feedback_loop_layout`, but the current source-read debt still comes from compute layout breaks (`source_fused_state rp/read/color/general/resolve=6052/0/18162/0/18162` in the latest moving run). A legal renderpass-local replacement would need a new graphics/subpass helper that can read the active MSAA attachment via feedback/input attachment semantics and write the texture-cache destination without compute or pass-ending barriers. Do not spend another loop on simple render-pass-key/input-id toggles.
- 2026-05-22 input-attachment source-local static gate: existing `VKRenderPass.cpp` can encode input attachments, but the hot Eternal Sonata fused source path is not a small render-pass-key patch. The valid moving profile's source-local CSV has six stable pairs, all `cached_dest=True`, `dst_render_target=False`, totaling `12,894` rows, so the fused blit writes texture-cache images, not framebuffer attachments. Current `m_current_renderpass_key` is built from `m_fbo_images` without input attachment ids in the normal draw path. A real renderpass-local path would need deeper shader/texture-cache plumbing or a pre-pass scratch/cache fill before the dependent render pass opens; do not spend a loop just adding input ids to the current FBO key.
- 2026-05-22 first-use source tag correlation: in the valid moving profile, steady 60-frame intervals have `source_fused_tag_new == source_fused_state_rp == resolve_break_blit_src` (`120/120/120` after startup), with repeats at `240`. That means the first consumer use of each hot source tag is the render-pass-breaking use; there is no earlier non-RP consumer repeat to reuse cheaply. A pre-stage design needs a producer-side or pre-renderpass signal, while a renderpass-local design needs real shader/texture-cache plumbing.
- 2026-05-22 producer-prestage static gate: the obvious producer hooks are not enough for a fast path. `surface_store::on_write(...)` runs per draw and earlier profiling showed millions of hot writer hits versus about `4,298` useful consumer source tags; `on_write_fast(...)` only updates `last_use_tag` and `require_resolve`. `texture_cache::lock_memory_region(...)` attaches the active framebuffer-storage section before/while it is used as an FBO and does not resolve/cache completed MSAA content. `flush_if_cache_miss_likely(...)` is CPU/Cell-memory synchronization, not the hot GPU source-cache fill. A pre-stage prototype needs a new profile hook that proves a coalesced "hot source is done, before first consumer blit opens/breaks a pass" moment, or this RSX lane should pivot to deeper shader plumbing or non-RSX codegen/HLE.
- 2026-05-20 Windows screenshot reliability fix: `tools/windows_rpcs3_lab.ps1` now raises the RPCS3 window foreground/topmost before window screenshots. One vertex/index scout run (`20260520-124609-rsx-auditor-vertex-index-field-windows`) captured the wrong browser window and must not be used for visual proof; use the corrected later runs instead.
- 2026-05-20 vertex/index upload scout: local `rpcs3-upstream` RSX auditor now logs `vertex_upload(draws/cache_hit/cache_miss/persistent_mb/volatile_mb)` and `index_upload(draws/emulated/restart/mb)`, and the summarizer reports these as `vertex-index-upload` pressure. Clean field run `20260520-125838-rsx-auditor-vertex-index-field-corrected-windows` on `\\.\DISPLAY2` reached clean field visuals and logged, over `8100` frames, about `6,132.92 MB` actual persistent vertex uploads, `513.49 MB` volatile vertex uploads, and `1,071.74 MB` index uploads. This is not a speed win yet, but it is now the strongest CPU-to-GPU migration target: design a verify-first GPU-resident vertex/index staging/cache or batched conversion path, not another present-upload or cached-transfer fanout tweak.
- 2026-05-20 shape-profile follow-up: `RPCS3_ES_RSX_RESOLVE=profile` now also emits shape-bucket vertex/index upload profile rows, exported by `tools/summarize_eternal_sonata_rsx_auditor.ps1`. Clean field run `20260520-133323-rsx-vertex-index-shape-profile-field-windows` had zero profile overflow. Top CPU geometry prep shapes were volatile draw-array strips `cmd/prim=2/6` with `attr=0x0109` stride `20` (`4,355,932` draws, `398.12 MB` volatile) and persistent indexed strips `cmd/prim=3/6`, `attr=0x010d`, stride `24` (`500,324` draws, `2,743.06 MB` persistent, hit/miss `172,289/328,035`). Index work collapsed to one normal u16 indexed-strip shape: `cmd/prim=3/6`, type `1/2`, `912,542` draws, `1,084.82 MB`. Next GPU move should verify a GPU-resident vertex/index staging/conversion path for these exact shapes; still not a speed win or Thor gate.
- 2026-05-20 index GPU byte-swap proof: `rpcs3-upstream` has a title-gated diagnostic path `RPCS3_ES_RSX_INDEX_UPLOAD=gpu-swap`, exposed as `-RsxIndexUpload GpuSwap` / `-WindowsRsxIndexUpload GpuSwap`. Clean field run `20260520-145038-rsx-index-gpu-swap-field-r2-windows` moved the dominant native u16 index endian conversion to Vulkan compute (`index_gpu_convert=893,565 dispatches / 1,046.37 MB`, about `6,619` dispatches and `7.75 MB` per 60 frames) with a clean late-field screenshot and no fatal/Vulkan errors. This is real `gpu-migration-credit`, but not a speed lane: per-draw compute caused `1,787,301` buffer barriers and `875,593` buffer render-pass breaks (`879,561` total RP breaks). Keep the switch default-off and diagnostic only. The next valid geometry offload must cache or batch repeated vertex/index work; do not promote one-compute-dispatch-per-draw to Thor or count it toward the 200% gate.
- 2026-05-20 frame-local cached index GPU byte-swap proof: `RPCS3_ES_RSX_INDEX_UPLOAD=gpu-swap-cached`, exposed as `-RsxIndexUpload GpuSwapCached` / `-WindowsRsxIndexUpload GpuSwapCached`, reuses same-frame native u16 index conversions by source address, size, primitive, count, and content hash. Clean field run `20260520-151947-rsx-index-gpu-swap-cached-field-windows` on `\\.\DISPLAY2` hit `368,091` frame-local cache reuses (`435.91 MB`) and cut GPU index compute dispatches to `521,646` (`609.92 MB`) versus the raw `893,565`. It also cut buffer barriers from `1,787,301` to `1,043,463`, buffer render-pass breaks from `875,593` to `505,761`, total render-pass breaks from `879,561` to `509,709`, and barrier-tracked range from `3,808.24 MB` to `2,443.96 MB`. The late-field screenshot was clean and the error scan had no fatal/crash/Vulkan/validation hit. Classify this as improved `gpu-migration-credit`, not a speed win: the run is capped near 30 FPS and still leaves huge per-draw geometry/barrier debt. Keep the switch default-off and Windows-only; the next proof should be matched uncapped/CPU-constrained A/B or a persistent/batched vertex/index cache that avoids dispatching inside the draw stream.
- 2026-05-20 high-cap index cache A/B: with auditor off and `-WindowsFrameLimit 240 -WindowsVblankRate 240`, `Off` run `20260520-152540-rsx-index-off-uncap240-field-windows` reached clean field at about `119.7 FPS` overlay (`119.96` title bar), while `GpuSwapCached` run `20260520-152853-rsx-index-gpuswapcached-uncap240-field-windows` reached the same field at about `109.3 FPS` overlay (`112.89` title bar). `GpuSwapCached` lowered overlay CPU utilization (`30.5%` total versus `38.8%`) and host RPCS3 CPU (`36.3%` versus `38.1%`) but raised GPU engine pressure (`92.6%` versus `85.9%`) and lost roughly `6%`-`9%` FPS. Classification: still `gpu-migration-credit` and `parked-speed-path`, not `windows-micro-win`, not a 200% gate candidate. Four-core constrained follow-up is currently `not-comparable`: cached reached field (`20260520-153619-cpu4-rsx-index-gpuswapcached-uncap240-field-windows`, about `33.3 FPS`), but `Off` controls ended at load-complete/Now Loading, so do not claim a smaller-machine win from that pair.
- 2026-05-20 discarded trust-cache probe: a temporary `GpuSwapCachedTrust` build skipped the per-hit content hash for same-frame index-cache hits. Capped/audited run `20260520-155514-rsx-index-gpu-swap-cached-trust-field-windows` stayed visually clean and had essentially the same cache shape (`369,483` hits, `521,381` dispatches), but high-cap run `20260520-155932-rsx-index-gpuswapcachedtrust-uncap240-field-windows` fell to about `95.9 FPS` overlay (`95.28` title bar), worse than hashed `GpuSwapCached` and much worse than `Off`. The trust mode was removed from source and wrappers after the test; do not resurrect it without a verifier. The kept build supports only `Off`, `GpuSwap`, and `GpuSwapCached`.
- 2026-05-20 vertex superset cache proof: `RPCS3_ES_RSX_VERTEX_SUPERSET_CACHE=profile|fast`, exposed as `-RsxVertexSupersetCache Profile|Fast` / `-WindowsRsxVertexSupersetCache`, reuses same-frame contained persistent vertex ranges from already uploaded GPU buffer slices. Profile run `20260520-173248-rsx-vertex-superset-profile-field-windows` found `60,600` contained-range opportunities (`544.05 MB`). Fast run `20260520-173648-rsx-vertex-superset-fast-field-windows` stayed visually clean and logged `76,836` hits (`751.17 MB`), reducing persistent vertex upload from `6,157.42 MB` to `5,619.05 MB` versus Profile. Matched high-cap A/B with auditor off kept the same about-120 FPS plateau: Off `20260520-174117-rsx-vertex-superset-off-uncap240-field-windows` at title `119.99` / overlay `120.03`, Fast `20260520-174435-rsx-vertex-superset-fast-uncap240-field-windows` at title `119.80` / overlay `119.39`. The final host sample improved from RPCS3 CPU `42.7%` / GPU sum `80.3%` to RPCS3 CPU `36.7%` / GPU sum `68.8%`, but this is a single field sample, not a 200% gate and not yet a durable speed win. Follow-up Fast proof `20260520-175802-rsx-vertex-superset-fast-menu-windows` reached a clean Pause overlay over field and logged `52,972` hits (`517.87 MB`); `20260520-180215-rsx-vertex-superset-fast-battle-windows` reached the tutorial prompt and active first-battle UI cleanly, logging `212,754` hits (`3,569.62 MB`) over `13,200` auditor frames. Classify as real RSX `gpu-migration-credit` plus provisional Windows CPU-load micro-win candidate, still not a 200% speed proof; only the exact field/pause/battle coverage is proven, and full menu/Options plus repeated matched A/B remain required before any Thor port discussion.
- 2026-05-20 vertex superset scan-depth follow-up: local `rpcs3-upstream` now accepts `RPCS3_ES_RSX_VERTEX_SUPERSET_SCAN=N`, exposed as `-RsxVertexSupersetScanLimit` / `-WindowsRsxVertexSupersetScanLimit`, to profile wider contained-range searches without changing the default `64` scan limit. Profile run `20260520-203542-rsx-vertex-superset-profile-scan256-field-windows` reached clean field and saw about `476` hits / `4.27 MB` per 60 frames, only modestly above the old 64-scan profile (`446` / `4.00 MB`). Fast smoke `20260520-203916-rsx-vertex-superset-fast-scan256-uncap240-field-windows` was visually clean but about `118.94` title / `117.70` overlay FPS, slightly below the prior 64-scan fast high-cap run. Keep default scan depth `64`; treat `256` as a diagnostic knob, not a speed win.
- 2026-05-20 volatile vertex cache proof: `RPCS3_ES_RSX_VERTEX_VOLATILE_CACHE=profile|fast`, exposed as `-RsxVertexVolatileCache Profile|Fast` / `-WindowsRsxVertexVolatileCache`, caches same-frame transient vertex blobs by actual source bytes and reuses an already uploaded GPU slice. Profile field run `20260520-182520-rsx-vertex-volatile-profile-field-windows` found `240,239` hits / `5,747,418` misses (`21.08 MB` hit bytes) with `551.96 MB` volatile vertex upload. Fast field run `20260520-182927-rsx-vertex-volatile-fast-field-windows` stayed visually clean and logged `239,839` hits (`21.07 MB`), reducing volatile upload to `528.09 MB`. Matched high-cap field A/B was not a speed win: Off `20260520-183334-rsx-vertex-volatile-off-uncap240-field-windows` reached title `118.63` / overlay `119.55` FPS, while Fast `20260520-183657-rsx-vertex-volatile-fast-uncap240-field-windows` reached title `109.69` / overlay `110.19` FPS. The late host sample shifted load from CPU toward GPU (`rpcs3` CPU `69.4%` to `37.5%`, GPU sum `68.3%` to `72.5%`), but the FPS loss makes this `gpu-migration-credit` / `cpu-load-tradeoff`, not `windows-micro-win`, not a Thor gate candidate. Keep default-off and Windows-only.
- 2026-05-20 persistent vertex cross-frame scout: local `rpcs3-upstream` now accepts `RPCS3_ES_RSX_VERTEX_PERSISTENT_CACHE=profile`, exposed as `-RsxVertexPersistentCache Profile` / `-WindowsRsxVertexPersistentCache Profile`. It hashes exact persistent/interleaved vertex source ranges only in profile mode and reports `vertex_persistent_cache(hit/change/new/hit_mb)` to test whether current per-frame cache misses could become a real long-lived GPU-resident vertex cache. Clean Windows field run `20260520-220710-rsx-vertex-persistent-profile-windows` on `\\.\DISPLAY2` used `DepthReadOnly + FastSampled + KeepReadOnly + Present GpuSwap + VertexSuperset Fast` and logged `1,807,018` stable cross-frame exact hits, `0` changed ranges, `482` new ranges, and `21,638.50 MB` stable hit bytes over `23,340` auditor frames, about `4,645` hits / `55.63 MB` per 60 frames. Screenshot `screenshot-0140s.png` was visually clean, but the full-byte hashing dragged field FPS to about `95`, so this is a strong architecture scout, not a speed win. Next Windows-only step should be a verify-first dedicated persistent vertex cache heap with real invalidation/eviction; do not simply keep the existing ring-cache entries past `purge()`, because ring offsets can be released and overwritten.
- 2026-05-21 combined RSX GPU-credit stack: audited field run `20260521-175655-rsx-combined-gpu-credit-field-windows` with `DepthReadOnly + FastSampled + KeepReadOnly + GpuSwap + Host DMA fence + VertexSuperset Fast + VertexPersistent Fast + IndexPersistent Fast` moved substantial RSX-consumed work into GPU-resident paths: `1,358,389` RSX-local credit events over `5,940` frames, persistent vertex fast `669,194` hits / `7,273.88 MB`, persistent index fast `660,750` hits / `775.91 MB`, sampled fused blit resolves `8,820`, and one GPU present upload / `3.52 MB`. Field screenshots were clean, and a no-auditor rerun `20260521-182002-cpu4-combined-fast-rerun-field-windows` was field-clean on `\\.\DISPLAY2`, but it is not a speed win: CPU4 idle field was flat-to-lower versus Off, and early moving-loop attempts `20260521-182603-cpu4-combined-fast-movingloop-rerun-windows` and `20260521-183034-cpu4-combined-fast-movingloop-longsettle-windows` stayed on loading/black frames instead of valid moving gameplay. The delayed-confirm route supersedes that blocker: Off route `20260521-200247-cpu4-off-movingloop-longsettle-routecheck-windows`, geometry-only `20260521-204333-cpu4-geometry-only-movingloop-delayed-confirm-windows`, resolve/depth/present-only `20260521-210440-cpu4-resolve-depth-present-movingloop-delayed-confirm-windows`, and full combined `20260521-210934-cpu4-combined-fast-movingloop-delayed-confirm-windows` all reached clean field start/movement/late screenshots with empty error scans. Treat the older loading/black captures as route-timing invalid/timing-sensitive failed attempts, not the current combined-stack blocker. Keep this stack as `gpu-migration-credit`, Windows-only, default-off, and not a 200% gate candidate. Next useful speed proof is a matched delayed-confirm moving-loop A/B or a new coarse render-pass-local/SPU-codegen job, not more black-frame bisecting.

- Target handheld: AYN Thor Base/Pro/Max.
- Known ADB model string: `AYN_Thor`.
- Debug APK path after assemble: `app\build\outputs\apk\debug\rpcsx-thor-experiment-debug.apk`.
- Android package: `net.rpcsx.easy`.
- Launcher label: `RPCSX for AYN Thor Experiment`.
- Launcher activity: `net.rpcsx.MainActivity`.
- This fork sets `BuildConfig.FORK_BUILD=true`; automatic upstream UI/core update prompts should stay disabled.
- Folder import is intentionally conservative: only loose `.pkg` and `.edat` files are sent to the native installer. Loose `.iso` files under Android external-storage documents are added as direct library entries instead of extracted, because the current core can abort while extracting some ISO directory entries.
- External ISO entries parse `PS3_GAME/PARAM.SFO` and `PS3_GAME/ICON0.PNG` directly from the ISO to populate title IDs, names, cheat matching, and cached cover art.
- The README is source-first: no big public APK download button, no support queue positioning.
- In-game Back is handled by `RPCSXActivity`; it opens the native RPCSX Home Menu on a background thread and then acts as menu back/resume while the menu is up.
- Gameplay Back behavior is hardcoded for Thor/fork builds: Android Back / Thor Back opens the native Home Menu, the core forces pause-during-Home-Menu on, and another Back press injects the native menu back/cancel action so nested pages back up and the root menu resumes gameplay.
- Gameplay-only physical hotkeys are hardcoded for Thor controls: `Select + R1` toggles Fast Forward 2x, `Select + right stick down` quick-saves, and `Select + right stick up` quick-loads the latest per-game savestate. Select is delayed and sent as a normal tap only when no hotkey combo is completed.

Install and launch:

```powershell
& "$env:ANDROID_HOME\platform-tools\adb.exe" devices -l
& "$env:ANDROID_HOME\platform-tools\adb.exe" install -r app\build\outputs\apk\debug\rpcsx-thor-experiment-debug.apk
& "$env:ANDROID_HOME\platform-tools\adb.exe" shell pm grant net.rpcsx.easy android.permission.POST_NOTIFICATIONS
& "$env:ANDROID_HOME\platform-tools\adb.exe" shell monkey -p net.rpcsx.easy 1
```

If the launcher or another foreground app steals focus, launch the main activity directly:

```powershell
& "$env:ANDROID_HOME\platform-tools\adb.exe" shell am start -n net.rpcsx.easy/net.rpcsx.MainActivity
```

If the app does not appear, verify the installed package:

```powershell
& "$env:ANDROID_HOME\platform-tools\adb.exe" shell pm list packages net.rpcsx.easy
& "$env:ANDROID_HOME\platform-tools\adb.exe" shell cmd package resolve-activity --brief net.rpcsx.easy
& "$env:ANDROID_HOME\platform-tools\adb.exe" shell dumpsys activity activities | Select-String -Pattern 'topResumedActivity|net.rpcsx.easy'
& "$env:ANDROID_HOME\platform-tools\adb.exe" shell pidof net.rpcsx.easy
```

## Native Home Menu / OSD Work

- Android already shows the native RPCSX in-game `Home Menu` over gameplay. Do not build a separate Android/Compose OSD unless the user explicitly asks for a replacement.
- The Android wrapper currently opens that menu through `_rpcsx_openHomeMenu`, surfaced as `RPCSX.instance.openHomeMenu()`. `RPCSXActivity` treats Android/Thor Back as the Home Menu toggle/back control; do not remap controller Circle/B to this Android-only action.
- Adding first-class menu rows such as `Cheats` or `Show FPS` belongs in the native/core Home Menu implementation, or behind a deliberate exported C ABI hook that the Android app can call. Android layout files cannot directly add rows to the existing menu.
- Native Home Menu source now lives in this repo under `app/src/main/cpp/rpcsx/rpcs3/Emu/RSX/Overlays/HomeMenu/`.
- The Android-side native export surface for the full core lives at `app/src/main/cpp/rpcsx/android/src/rpcsx-android.cpp`; the lightweight dynamic-loader wrapper lives at `app/src/main/cpp/native-lib.cpp`.
- Localized Home Menu IDs live in `app/src/main/cpp/rpcsx/rpcs3/Emu/localized_string_id.h`.
- FPS/performance overlay rendering/reset code lives around `app/src/main/cpp/rpcsx/rpcs3/Emu/RSX/Overlays/overlay_perf_metrics.cpp`.
- The vendored Home Menu now has a `Cheats` page source at `app/src/main/cpp/rpcsx/rpcs3/Emu/RSX/Overlays/HomeMenu/overlay_home_menu_cheats.cpp`, plus top-level `Fast Forward 2x` and `Show FPS` toggles after Resume/Cheats.
- These vendored Home Menu changes require the source-built/bundled core APK or another core build that includes these files. The default lightweight-wrapper build alone cannot change the native Home Menu.
- The current Home Menu on this fork should include `Resume Game`, `Cheats`, `Fast Forward 2x`, `Show FPS`, `Settings`, `Trophies`, `Take Screenshot`, `Start/Stop Recording`, `SaveState`, `Restart Game`, and `Exit Game`.
- FPS display is already represented in the RPCSX config file under `Video -> Performance Overlay -> Enabled`. Through the Android settings bridge this should be treated as `Video@@Performance Overlay@@Enabled`.
- If only a simple FPS toggle is requested, prefer toggling `Performance Overlay.Enabled` first. Avoid enabling debug overlays or graph-heavy performance views unless the user asks for more metrics.
- Fast Forward 2x is a runtime guest-time speedhack: `_rpcsx_setFastForwardEnabled` changes `Core -> Clocks scale` to `200` and restores the previous value when disabled. It is not a frame-limit uncap, and it may break timing-sensitive games.
- Hotkey save/load uses `_rpcsx_saveState` and `_rpcsx_loadState`; quick-save forces continuous savestate mode so gameplay resumes instead of saving-and-exiting.
- Cheat toggles in the Home Menu should show only the currently running game's cheats, one row per cheat, with controller-friendly toggle behavior.
- Cheat state is persisted in `config/patch_config.yml`; generated patch definitions live under `config/patches/TITLEID_patch.yml`.
- For live cheat toggling, use the existing optional native hook `RPCSX.setPatchEnabled(hash, description, enabled)` when the current core exports it. If the hook is missing or the patch cannot apply live, the menu must clearly say the toggle takes effect after restart.
- Keep OSD wording user-facing: say `Cheats`, `Show FPS`, `Restart needed`, or `Needs first boot`; avoid database/internal words in the menu.

## Cheat Work

- Support offline single-player cheats only.
- Do not help bypass DRM, anti-cheat, or online/multiplayer protections.
- Bundled Aldos/Artemis source lives under `app/src/main/assets/cheats`.
- Bundled SQLite cheat database lives at `app/src/main/assets/cheats/cheats.db`; regenerate it with `python tools/build_cheat_db.py` after changing bundled NCL/imported patch assets.
- Chidreams/RPCS3-ready imported patches live under `app/src/main/assets/cheats/chidreams` and are stored in `cheats.db` as `rpcs3_patch` entries.
- Converted test fixtures live under `app/src/test/resources/cheats/converted`.
- RPCSX/RPCS3 patches require a learned PPU hash; boot a game once, close it, then install fixed-write cheats.
- RPCS3-ready `rpcs3_patch` entries already include PPU hashes and can be installed without the Artemis conversion step.
- AoB cheats are parsed and counted as risky, but should not be installed until native byte validation/scanning exists.

## Recommended Game Settings

- Bundled database: `app/src/main/assets/config/config_database.dat`.
- Current source endpoint: `https://api.rpcs3.net/config/?api=v1`.
- The snapshot is RPCS3-style JSON: `games[TITLEID].config` contains a YAML config snippet.
- Android-side manager: `app/src/main/java/net/rpcsx/config/GameSettingsDatabase.kt`.
- The app seeds and reads a writable local cache at `config/GuiConfigs/config_database.dat` under the RPCSX root so a future/core-side config database reader can find the familiar upstream location.
- Preserve a valid local cache when it is newer than or equal to the bundled APK snapshot; do not stomp refreshed cache data during app startup.
- The game detail card has a refresh icon that downloads `https://api.rpcs3.net/config/?api=v1` into the local cache. Startup should stay offline-friendly and only seed/fallback to the bundled snapshot.
- The game detail screen shows this as `Recommended Settings`, not as database jargon.
- Managed per-game configs are written to `config/custom_configs/config_TITLEID.yml` with the `# RPCSX_THOR_AUTO_SETTINGS` header.
- Never overwrite an existing custom config unless it has the managed header. User-created custom configs win.
- Boot flow applies the recommended config before launch only when the game has a title-ID match and the user has not turned the switch off for that game.

## Per-Game Cache Workflow

- Android-side cache status lives at `app/src/main/java/net/rpcsx/performance/GameCacheRepository.kt`.
- Game detail reads `cache/cache/TITLEID` under `RPCSX.rootDirectory`, counts PPU/SPU/shader entries, shows cache size, and exposes refresh/clear controls.
- RPCSX/RPCS3 stores RSX shader cache below the PPU cache path (`.../ppu-*/shaders_cache/`), so the same per-game compiled-cache root must cover PPU, SPU, and shaders.
- Compiled-cache storage selection lives at `app/src/main/java/net/rpcsx/performance/CacheStorageManager.kt` and is exposed from Settings as `Compiled Cache Storage`.
- The core still expects `RPCSX.rootDirectory/cache/cache`; SD-card selection redirects that app-owned compiled-cache path with a symlink to the selected app-owned external-files directory, covering PPU/SPU/shader cache together.
- Do not offer arbitrary SAF folders for emulator cache until the native core supports URI/document access or a stable cache-directory setting. App-owned storage roots are the safe selectable locations.
- Switching compiled-cache storage can migrate existing cache data and should warn users that SD card cache may be slower and large moves can take minutes.
- The native wrapper has an optional `_rpcsx_preparePpuCache` hook surfaced as `RPCSX.supportsPpuCachePreparation()` and `RPCSX.preparePpuCache(...)`.
- The bundled source-built Thor core exposes `_rpcsx_preparePpuCache`; downloaded/older external cores may still lack it, so keep `supportsPpuCachePreparation()` as the UI gate.
- Do not call private C++ internals like `Emulator::BootGame` or `ppu_precompile` across the wrapper boundary unless the core intentionally exports a stable C ABI.
- Library delete now clears title-ID cache when a title ID is known; keep ISO/file-name fallback for entries without metadata.

## Thor Variant Notes

The active device is Thor Max, but Base, Pro, and Max share the same CPU/GPU performance target:

| Variant | CPU/GPU | RAM | Storage | App strategy |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Base | Snapdragon 8 Gen 2 / Adreno 740 | 8 GB LPDDR5X | 128 GB UFS 4.0 | Same CPU/GPU presets, smaller cache budget, avoid memory-hungry global views. |
| Pro | Snapdragon 8 Gen 2 / Adreno 740 | 12 GB LPDDR5X | 256 GB UFS 4.0 | Default comfort target for testing. |
| Max | Snapdragon 8 Gen 2 / Adreno 740 | 16 GB LPDDR5X | 1 TB UFS 4.0 | Active proof device; more cache/library/logging headroom; do not assume faster PPU compile or make Max-only memory behavior the default. |

- Treat Base as the memory floor for public presets, Pro as the comfort target, and Max as the fastest lab/truth device for this project. If a cache, preload, shader, or logging experiment is expected to exceed Base headroom, mark it `max-only` or `pro-max` in the experiment record before testing.
- Thor Lite is Snapdragon 865 / Adreno 650 and is not the PS3 performance target for this fork. Do not apply Snapdragon 8 Gen 2 affinity masks to Lite.

## Thor CPU Notes

The connected AYN Thor reports board/platform `kalama` and this CPU part layout:

- CPUs `0-2`: Cortex-A510, part `0xd46`, mask `0x07`
- CPUs `3-4`: Cortex-A715, part `0xd4d`, mask `0x18`
- CPUs `5-6`: Cortex-A710, part `0xd47`, mask `0x60`
- CPU `7`: Cortex-X3, part `0xd4e`, mask `0x80`
- Heavy work mask for performance plus prime cores: CPUs `3-7`, mask `0xF8`

Use detected topology for presets. Do not assume every Snapdragon 8 Gen 2 device orders logical CPUs the same way.

## Thor Optimization Notes

- Already cleaned up Android-side hotspots: folder scan queues use `ArrayDeque`, URI file copy uses a larger stream buffer, ISO metadata avoids duplicate directory parsing, patch status file checks are cached, and `games.json` saves are debounced.
- Current Thor compile preset lives at `app/src/main/java/net/rpcsx/performance/ThorPerformanceProfile.kt`.
- The preset is applied on AYN/Thor/kalama targets: `Max LLVM Compile Threads=2`, `LLVM Precompilation=false`, `SPU Cache=true`, `SPU Decoder=Recompiler (LLVM)`, `Use LLVM CPU=cortex-a78`, native scheduler enabled when affinity succeeds, and on-disk shader cache enabled.
- Do not reintroduce an Android startup override that writes `Use LLVM CPU = cortex-a34`; that silently downgrades Thor JIT codegen and can undo the profile on later launches. Do not let this vendored LLVM use `cortex-a510`, `cortex-a710`, `cortex-a715`, or `cortex-x3` on Thor unless Android reports SVE: those Armv9 CPU definitions enable SVE/SVE2 in LLVM while Snapdragon 8 Gen 2 reports dotprod/i8mm/bf16 but not SVE.
- Do not allow Thor per-game recommended configs to select `SPU Decoder: Recompiler (ASMJIT)`. ASMJIT is an x64 SPU backend; ARM64 should use the LLVM SPU recompiler.
- Native wrapper affinity helper: `RPCSX.setProcessAffinityMask(0xF8)` pins current app/native threads to Thor CPUs `3-7` where Android permits it. MainActivity applies it before core initialization so early native threads can inherit the performance-core mask. This is a first-pass compile relief, not a replacement for native PPU/SPU/RSX per-class affinity.
- The first `Thor Feature Doctor` slice lives in `_rpcsx_systemInfo()` inside `app/src/main/cpp/rpcsx/android/src/rpcsx-android.cpp`; the existing System Info dialog now reports configured LLVM CPU, fallback CPU, AArch64 per-core names, and Android HWCAP/HWCAP2 feature flags.
- Custom GPU driver UI lives at `app/src/main/java/net/rpcsx/ui/drivers/GpuDriversScreen.kt`.
- Curated GPU driver channels live in `app/src/main/java/net/rpcsx/ui/channels/UpdateChannelsScreen.kt`; keep K11MCH1/Kimchi first, StevenMXZ experimental, and CI builds clearly marked risky.
- Thor Base/Pro/Max are Adreno 740. Prefer A6xx/A7xx Turnip packages when testing custom drivers, keep `Default` as fallback, and label A8xx/Gen8 packages as not for Thor.
- On-screen controls preference lives in `ControllerOverlayPrefs`; Thor/AYN/kalama targets default hidden, non-Thor devices default visible.
- Sixaxis motion preference lives in `SixaxisMotionPrefs`, Android sensor capture lives in `SixaxisMotionController`, and the JNI wrapper looks for `_rpcsx_overlayPadMotionData`. Current downloaded cores may not export it; keep the matching core patch in `core-patches/` until core builds include it.
- Next low-risk Android work: cache cheat badge lookups per game title ID, add stale-cache/core-version labeling, and keep heavy global cheat expansion off Base unless requested.
- Next native/core work: benchmark/iterate the Thor PPU/SPU/RSX affinity split, add authoritative cache status, and continue SPU common recompiler parity. UI-only changes cannot truly pin native compile threads.
- Default PPU compile experiment for Base/Pro/Max: Max LLVM compile threads `2`, full PPU precompile off, heavy mask `0xF8`; benchmark higher worker counts only as an opt-in cache-builder experiment with memory logs running.
- 2026-05-12 crash note: the rtime-keyed reservation notifier port from `d638a9ec2` was backed out after Eternal Sonata hit a native `SIGBUS BUS_ADRALN` in `PPU[0x1000000]` with PC/LR `0xd0020d77` and repeated `MemoryRead: PC is 0x0` logs. Capture lives at `debug-captures/20260512-233228-game-crash`. Prefs confirmed the APK bundled core was loaded, not the stale `files/librpcsx-android_armv8-a_v20251011-e27926d.so`. Re-port this area in smaller gates, starting with detection/logging before changing wait/notify behavior again.

- Fixture source: `app/src/main/assets/cheats/ncl/1417_Odin Sphere Leifthrasir BLUS31601 v01.01 av01.00.ncl`
- Converted output:
  - `app/src/test/resources/cheats/converted/odin_sphere_leifthrasir_blus31601_patch.yml`
  - `app/src/test/resources/cheats/converted/odin_sphere_leifthrasir_blus31601_patch_config.yml`
